member of the commission. – mr president, i have listened carefully to all the views and the concerns you shared. the commission shares your views on the decisive role of energy efficiency in accelerating our transition to climate neutrality and in phasing out russian fossil fuels so that our security of supply is guaranteed and prices are kept under control. indeed, many challenges lie ahead, and this winter will not be the end of them. we need a european solution that will last, based on solidarity and joint effort. the vote for the ambitious report on the energy efficiency directive recast proposal will be part of this solution. indeed, the recast energy efficiency directive will set the framework for actions which are urgently needed. the proposed amendments on energy efficiency targets, as well as the concrete measures to achieve them, appear to me as a good start for the next steps in the negotiations. let me finish by thanking you again for your work on such an important proposal. i do hope that we can see the adoption of the recast eed as a strong eu contribution to support our economy and society as swiftly as possible. having seen the constructive discussions in parliament, and also in the council, i am very much looking forward to finalising the process.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on wednesday 14 september 2022.
the next item on the agenda is the joint debate on the report by isabel carvalhais, on behalf of the committee on fisheries, on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council amending regulation (eu) 2019/833 laying down conservation and control measures applicable to the regulatory area of the northwest atlantic fisheries organisation (com (2022) 0051 c9-0046/2022 2022/0035 (cod)) (a90198/2022) and the report by isabel carvalhais, on behalf of the committee on fisheries, on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing conservation and management measures applicable to the area covered by the agreement on fisheries in the western and central pacific and amending council regulation (ec) no 520/2007 (com (2021) 0198 c915/03 2021/2021 2021/103 (cod) 009000 (a920/92).
rapporteur. mr president, commissioner, i would first like to recall that on 16 february this year the commission presented a proposal to amend regulation (eu) 2019/833 in order to transpose into european union law the conservation and enforcement measures adopted by the northwest atlantic fisheries organisation, to which the european union has been a contracting party since 1979. the main objective of this proposal is to transpose into union law the amendments to the conservation and enforcement measures adopted by nafo at its annual meeting in september 2021. these include, for example, improvements to the wording of nafo and adapting its wording to the legal context of the european union, as well as transposing the revised provisions on additional procedures and serious infringements related to the use of certain fishing gear. in this context, i consider that the european commission's proposals fully comply with the principles for a rapid transposition process into union law and therefore no amendments have been tabled. in another file, the european commission submitted on 23 march 2021 a proposal to incorporate into union law the conservation and management measures adopted by the western and central pacific fisheries commission, which is responsible for the management of fishery resources in the western and central pacific, and which is responsible for adopting measures for the conservation and management of marine biological resources under its responsibility, which are binding on all contracting parties. the european union fleet authorised and licensed to operate in the convention area is quite small, but the quantities caught are considerable and have a significant impact on the management of these resources. the importance of technical measures and monitoring of the use of fish aggregating devices and of measures to protect certain sensitive species should therefore be stressed. i also believe that the european fisheries control agency (efca) should be involved in the data transmission scheme between the convention secretariat, the member states, the masters of european union vessels and the european commission. the european fisheries control agency is the union agency that coordinates national operational activities in the fisheries area and assists member states in their implementation of the common fisheries policy, so we really need to start including efca more in all these processes. i would also like to stress, in conclusion, the importance of including the annexes to the regulation to be published in the official journal of the european union, which arises from the need to ensure legality within the limits of the treaties. i would remind you that the court of justice of the european union has already pointed out to us the need to include this in the list. in short, when adopting conservation and management measures taken by regional fisheries management organisations, the european union must ensure that the measures adopted are in line with the ambitious objectives set by the common fisheries policy, which means that, in order to ensure minimal impacts on habitats, resources and species, the common fisheries policy must be applied to all union fleets, regardless of their geographical area of operation.
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, dear ms carvalhais, i would like to start by thanking you for bringing the negotiations on this important transpositions to a successful end. let me briefly say a few words on both of them. the main purpose of the commission proposal regarding the northwest atlantic fisheries organization is to implement into eu law the most recent conservation and enforcement measures adopted by this organisation at its annual meeting in september 2021. as you know, such transposition of updated measures by the northwest atlantic fisheries organization is a regular legislative exercise. however, in addition, this commission proposal includes delegated powers to the commission concerning landing and inspection for greenland halibut, as well as the control measures for cod in division 3m. such delegated powers are important in order to ensure a fast transposition into eu law of these technical rules that change every year. the adoption of this amending regulation will allow union vessels to fish on the same footing as vessels from other contracting parties of the northwest atlantic fisheries organisation for future fishing seasons. this has been a recurrent request from many stakeholders across the eu, and i would like to thank the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs for their full support for the commission proposal. as regards the commission proposal for a regulation for the union fleet operating in the western and central pacific fisheries commission convention area, i believe that overall the amendments by the co-legislators improved the text. at the same time, those amendments are indeed in line with the organisation’s conservation and management measures. however, i regret that the co-legislators missed the opportunity to simplify the overall process of transposition, since some of the avenues proposed by the commission to streamline the process were not supported by the co—legislators, despite parliament’s and council’s earlier calls on the commission to further improve the process. i obviously fully respect your positions, but i think we missed a good opportunity here to simplify the procedures, which are currently too complex and long. the transposition process lasts on average 18 months – from the drafting of the proposal by the commission to its adoption by the co-legislators. considering the nature of this exercise, this is far too long and complicated. i would therefore like to ask for the inclusion of a statement on behalf of the commission in the minutes of this debate reflecting our disappointment. the commission will, in any case, continue to work together with the european parliament and the council on further improvements to the implementation of the process. in this particular context, i would like to point to the two commission proposals for the transposition of international fisheries conservation measures which are currently stalled in the interinstitutional negotiations. one proposal concerns the conservation of the atlantic bluefin tuna management plan, and the other the atlantic bluefin tuna catch documentation scheme. i am concerned that such delays affect the credibility of the european union in the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas. it is indeed difficult to explain to our international partners why it takes so long for the eu to implement its international obligations. i am also concerned that the lack of transposition has negative consequences for the eu fishing fleet. we should really avoid this. we trust that work will pick up in light of the most recent additional amendment proposed by the commission in april this year and, of course, i look forward to seeing these two files advanced quickly. statement by the commission(in writing)“the european parliament and the council underlined on several occasions the need to further improve the process of implementation of conservation measures adopted by regional fisheries management organisations (rfmos), notably in a timely manner. the commission regrets that its attempt to streamline the implementation process has not been fully retained by the european parliament and the council. the commission will continue to work together with the european parliament and the council on further improvements to the implementation process.”
on behalf of the ppe group mr president, commissioner, the resolutions of the regional fisheries management organisations which we are discussing today were agreed after lengthy negotiations, although the commission's proposal to transpose these agreements tends to go a little further. no one is against taking ambitious measures to manage fisheries. however, ambitious measures must be agreed and implemented by all. the fishermen of the european union should not always be the ones to pay the bill for these overly ambitious and unilateral measures of the union. the objective of having an exemplary european fleet is laudable, but our fleet is becoming less and less competitive. the more stringent the measures adopted by the european union, the more we depend on imported products. the agreements in the rfmos should be strictly transposed, ensuring a level playing field while promoting the high sustainability standards of the union for all foreign fleets.
on behalf of the renew group. – mr president, if we are to solve the climate crisis and the biodiversity crisis, we need healthy and resilient oceans and we need global solutions – and the union memberships in international regional fisheries—management organisations are key tools. today, we are debating two such organisations and the memberships that we have there, and we, together with the colleagues and the rapporteur, are now implementing better measures for both eu vessels and other vessels in the western and central pacific area. for me and renew, it was very important to the final outcome, besides ensuring correct implementation of the international obligations, that we also included everything being done in a timely manner, that we work to strive for strengthened ocean governance and promoting sustainable fishing management. we managed to secure just those three and also more. we also managed to strengthen control measures in relation to ocean governance and marine litter. i am very proud of the outcome and want to express full support from the renew team. thank you so much for the cooperation on this important file.
on behalf of the id group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, measures to conserve fisheries resources are necessary because the sustainability of stocks has been tested: in saint-pierre and miquelon, after over-exploitation by foreign trawlers in the 1970s, the collapse of cod stocks has been almost irreversible. our small-scale fisheries are the best able to adapt and react quickly to changes in the local resource. it is the first victim of the intensive fishing economic model that the european union has encouraged but has been unable to stop. this is a model of virtuous and sustainable fishing that ideologues have chosen to sacrifice for the benefit of multinational companies. if our leaders really cared about protecting our food sovereignty and a sustainable model of resource exploitation, they would stop undermining our fisheries and making regrettable decisions for which they then blame the people of the sea.
on behalf of the left group. mr president, this discussion is relevant to the integration of the rules on fishing zones managed through intergovernmental organisations or by international conventions into the legal rules on fisheries, which we are not opposed to as a rule. more relevant, however, are the conditions for each state to be able to access these fishing areas, some of which are of historical relevance, as is the case in the north-west atlantic, where some portuguese vessels still operate, with cod as one of the reference species. since the implementation of the common fisheries policy in portugal in 1986, the sector has not only suffered a drastic erosion but has also lost access to traditional fishermen abroad. this has restricted or prevented the portuguese fleet from accessing new fishing opportunities. a fisheries policy is needed which is based on the development of the sector in accordance with the needs of each state, making fishing opportunities available abroad, countering concentration phenomena and providing adequate support, in particular for the renewal of the fleet, and which gives value to fishermen and their incomes.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would first like to thank my colleague, mrs isabel carvalhais, and all those involved in this report for their good cooperation. this proposal for a regulation on the western and central pacific fisheries convention is of great importance both economically and environmentally and for the competitiveness of european fisheries. as a shadow rapporteur, i have set myself three essential objectives. firstly, to promote a balance between the different fleets operating in this part of the ocean. european actors must be defended. secondly, to ensure that such a regulation is implemented in time. and finally, to promote a science-based, innovation-based approach to all fisheries-related activities, improving practices, enabling new opportunities and promoting sustainability.
mr president, commissioner, dear rapporteur isabel carvalhais, the transposition into union law of the conservation and enforcement measures adopted by the northwest atlantic fisheries organisation is crucial to ensuring compliance with sustainable fishing practices in these waters. it is a pity that it has taken so long to transpose this. the union must speed up these processes by ensuring that fisheries conservation and recovery practices are implemented, in particular by minimising infringements, including illegal and unreported fishing, which harm both the environment and the union's fishermen who comply and whose activity is threatened by those who do not. at the same time, and it is not too much to reinforce, while ensuring these transpositions, it is crucial to equip the european fisheries control agency with the human and material resources to enable it to fight effectively against any illegal practice. in this, as in all organisations, the union must have a strong and active voice in environmental conservation.
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, i appreciate the discussion that we had today on the two proposals and i would like to thank again the rapporteur and all of those involved in the negotiations on both files. a couple of you – mr mato, mr gonzález and other honourable members –also made some more general observations on the role of our fishing fleet and on the need to develop management measures in close dialogue with them. i absolutely agree with you, and this is what we, member states, the european commission, with your support, are doing and the transposition of files we are discussing today are key for our fleet. that’s why for these reasons we need to join forces in the future to transpose all measures much faster into eu law, simplifying the process. so i hope that i can truly count on your support. honourable members, the adoption of both proposals will update the relevant regulations with the most recent conservation and management measures. of course, i hope that we can also make swift progress on the other outstanding rfmo implementation proposals and that we will keep working together towards improving the implementation process.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, thank you very much for your comments, which i have listened to with great attention. i would like to emphasize the following: what this parliament is asking for, and what we do in our work, is that the measures should always be of the utmost technical and legal rigour and that they should be precisely implemented in time, because we have fishermen practising their profession in the areas covered by these conventions and who need to know, to have guarantees, in this case legal guarantees, that what they are doing is right and therefore there must be transparency and simplicity in the measures so that they can be easily understood by everyone. and look, that's exactly what happened in the nafo file, it was a very quick transposition. in almost three months the transplant was done without any problems. now, what happened in the case of the western and central pacific convention was quite different, because there it was the commission itself, to our astonishment, that delayed the negotiation process by not showing any kind of openness - and there is a record which obviously shows that i am not being untrue here - and that it did not, in fact, allow the negotiation to proceed at the best possible pace, and therefore things are as they are. we thought our proposals were valid and i would therefore like to suggest, in a spirit of absolute openness and a constructive spirit, that there could also be a little more openness and flexibility on the part of the commission in these situations where we put forward our proposals, because i believe that together - parliament, the commission and the council - we can do much more to simplify these implementation processes without compromising their rigour and also by trying to improve what can be improved here, without therefore starting from the principle that this house is just a filler and that there is no room for improvement. we therefore seek to ensure the full applicability of the common fisheries policy and it is in this spirit that we will continue to work with the commission and the council on future dossiers.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on tuesday 13 september 2022.
the next item is the briefing by mr hannes heide on the impact of covid-19 closures on children and young people in the eu (2022/2004 (ini)) (a9-0216/2022).
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, let me start by stressing that the commission very much welcomes this very timely and important report. schools across the eu are now trying to address negative trends in students’ competence and mental health due to loss of in-person teaching and social distancing during the covid-19 crisis. this report will be instrumental in helping us direct efforts to support learning institutions across europe. i would like to assure you that the commission has been closely monitoring the situation and working with the member states in this regard. we have already taken several steps towards alleviating the negative impact that covid-19 closures of educational, cultural, youth and sports activities had on children and young people in the eu. as a result, three recently—published reference documents help member states to address those challenges. first, in november last year, the commission proposal for a council recommendation on blended learning for high quality and inclusive primary and secondary education was adopted by the council. the recommendation recognises that the physical, mental and emotional well—being of students was compromised during school closures. secondly, a detailed framework for the social and personal key competences – lifecomp – outlines the knowledge, skills and attitudes needed by young people to take care of their physical, social and mental well—being. third, on 30 june this year, the commission adopted a proposal for a council recommendation on pathways to school success. the goal of all these initiatives is to address our major concerns – the post—covid recovery, sustainability and under—achievement. this effort is part of our work on achieving the european education area by 2025. in fact, the new working group on schools under the european education area strategic framework for cooperation started its work already last year. in particular, pathways will be complemented by an expert group on school well—being to be set up in the coming months. when it comes to the budget, about 13% of the total rrf envelope, amounting to more than eur 63 billion, will be devoted to measures supporting education and skills. investment in digital education and skills will be around eur 28 billion. i would also like to stress that our erasmus+ programme has provided invaluable during the crisis. for instance, in 2020, the erasmus+ programme published two calls for proposals in the area of partnerships for digital education readiness and partnerships for creativity. each provided eur 100 million to respond to the educational challenges resulting from the covid—19 pandemic. with an overall indicative financial envelope of about eur 28 billion for 2021—2027, the programme is with nearly doubled funding compared to its predecessor, which opens additional opportunities. the programme also supports european online platforms for virtual cooperation and digital education, e-twinning and the school education gateway, which played a key role during the pandemic. in the field of sport and youth we also launched a number of initiatives like ‘be active at home’, the european week of sport, healthylifestyle4all and the european year of youth 2022. as for culture, a sector that also suffered so much, the commission published the communication ‘coronavirus: a common path to europe’s safe re—opening’. particularly useful in this context was the setting—up of creatives unite, a platform for and by the cultural and creative sectors. we made additional financial resources available through the coronavirus response investment initiative, support to mitigate unemployment risks in an emergency, the recovery and resilience facility, which can also be used for the cultural and creative sectors, react—eu and the future cohesion policy. the creative europe programme has an unprecedented budget of eur 2.4 billion up to 2027, and this is an increase of 60% compared with the previous programme. finally, we have worked a lot to encourage member states to consider culture as a strategic investment. as a result, at eu level, more than 2% of the total budget of the recovery and resilience plan will be dedicated to the cultural and creative sectors. mr president, honourable members, again i would like to applaud the efforts of the cult committee of the european parliament and particularly the work of the rapporteur, mr heide. our joint work is fundamental to help our young overcome the negative consequences of those two years. with your excellent collaboration i am sure we can continue doing more.
that concludes this item. the vote will take place on tuesday 13 september 2022. written declarations (article 171) the following documents shall be submitted to the court of justice:
in writing. the closure of educational, cultural, youth and sporting activities due to the measures taken to deal with the covid-19 pandemic has had a devastating impact on children and young people in the eu and the rest of the world. as acknowledged by neuropsychiatrists, childcare institutions and associations, these measures have had serious effects on mental health. it's gone from 10-20% of young people with mental health problems before the pandemic, to 25% in the last two years. a kind of "trigger" that has exacerbated existing fragility and that led, in the spring of 2021, to register 64% of young people between 18 and 34 years at risk of depression and unicef to note that suicide is now the second leading cause of death among young people. as the european parliament's intergroup on the rights of the child has long warned, the european union and the member states must take urgent and decisive measures to address and prevent these tragic phenomena, such as providing free and regular psychological support for teachers, educators and childcare professionals, as well as strengthening cultural and sporting activities that can positively impact this deep and widespread generational malaise.
in writing. there is no doubt that the closure of educational, cultural, youth and sports activities due to the pandemic has effectively deprived the younger generation of any form of socialization that was available to them. this has had horrifying consequences for the psycho-physical health of millions of children. we have an obligation to deal with these problems now, so as not to allow the course and development of these children to be affected. the report is a very important tool in shaping the policy interventions that need to be implemented at european level to give young people a real start in life. we must ensure that our young people receive the necessary psychological support in this process through appropriate support structures set up by the member states themselves. ensuring access to health, education, youth, culture and sport facilities during potential future crises should be a primary objective for the state.
the next item is the brief presentation of the interim report by mr olbrycht and mrs marques on the 2021 proposal for a revision of the multiannual financial framework (2021/0429r ((apf)) (a90227/2022).
the rapporteur. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: the fundamental question we are asking ourselves at this moment is whether the budget of the european union is prepared, whether it is properly structured, whether it is sufficient to meet the new challenges that lie ahead. the answer we give in the committee on budgets is that it is not prepared. we therefore have to review the multiannual financial framework and convince the council, that is to say the governments, but also the european commission, to make this kind of effort, to decide to show the public that we need changes in the budget. the first of these changes, which the european commission is now happily proposing with the support of the european parliament, is to make the legislative system more coherent. so on the one hand, we have to create new own income, including the appropriate use of emissions trading and the change of the model of this emissions trading. parliament has already taken a decision on this. at the same time, there is a second thread on energy issues, which is to say everything we voted on in our parliament under the slogan fit for 55. the idea was to have consistency between own income and new expenditure. and the third very important issue here is the question of the consequences of introducing certain legislative solutions, the consequences not for politicians, the consequences not for the european institutions, but the consequences for the people, for the europeans, for the people of our countries. so the idea was that in order to balance and reduce the negative consequences, we need to create an additional fund that would tie up the own income elements, but also the issues related to the achievement of climate goals. such a mechanism to protect citizens is crucial. what we're talking about today is not just a technical discussion. this conversation today is about the fact that we need to translate, translate the language of legislative decisions on energy and climate change into concrete action items for the european budget. that is why we are talking today about the social climate fund, about where the money for this fund is to come from, how to ensure that it is budgeted and controlled by the european parliament. we therefore have an important decision to make which will make our action more coherent. own income, fit for 55 and caring for the people together, this is going to lead to a meaningful and wisely shared social climate fund.
mr. chairman, there is no doubt that we are facing the unprecedented impact of many crises. this is a climate crisis, we're responding with the climate fund, of course, russia's aggression in ukraine. we support ukraine. it would be excellent news if ukraine could win as soon as possible with our help and that of other allies. the energy crisis, many states are still feeling the aftermath of the covid crisis. it is important to convince member states to be more responsive to the requirements that have been put forward here today. budgetary changes, i was a bit skeptical about the generation of own resources in the eu budget policy, but i think it really has obvious results. we're achieving the goals we set out to achieve. it is a very powerful instrument, the european budget. let's use it to truly protect the vulnerable, promote sustainable growth and push these new programs to the benefit of our citizens.
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, dear rapporteurs, the commission welcomes this interim report. it is based on the commission’s proposal of december 2021 and it provides important and timely input for a targeted amendment of the multiannual financial framework regulation. let me briefly recall two very specific objectives of the commission proposal. first, to allow the financing of the social climate fund, as proposed in july 2021, by increasing the relevant mff ceilings by the exact amounts of expenditure proposed for the social climate fund. secondly, in conjunction with the proposal of december 2021 for introducing new own resources to the eu budget in line with the interinstitutional agreement of december 2020, to enable the use of new own resources, once introduced, for the repayment of next generation eu borrowing, without harming the integrity of the multiannual financial framework and its programme. i want to thank the european parliament and, in particular, the rapporteurs for their support for the commission proposal, which fully reflects the principles of unity and universality of the budget. we take good note of the focused modifications suggested by parliament. we acknowledge that the amendment to the multiannual financial framework regulation is intrinsically linked to not only the new own resources proposal of december 2021, but also to sectoral legislative proposals, namely the social climate fund, the carbon border adjustment mechanism and the emissions trading system directive. on the latter, the trilogue negotiations between the european parliament and the council are still ongoing. the outcome of this legislative process will, of course, be decisive for the further consideration of this targeted amendment of the multiannual financial framework proposal. to close, let me once again thank parliament for this constructive interim report.
that concludes this item. the vote will take place on tuesday 13 september 2022.

on behalf of the verts/ale group. – madam president, tomorrow we will listen to the state of the union. we will hear an emphasis on the european values and the rule of law. solidarity with war refugees will be praised. but let me do a reality check. thousands of refugees are pushed back at our borders. greece, for instance, violates human rights on a daily basis and silences those who speak up. but the commission and the council stay silent as well, which almost invites other countries to push back refugees as well: croatia, hungary, poland, bulgaria, lithuania, and more. we have entered the stage where our european values have been rendered meaningless at our borders. and the netherlands and belgium keep their doors closed for hundreds of asylum seekers; they sleep on the streets, wait endlessly to make their asylum claim. these are two rich countries, which simply failed to organise themselves. it affects the refugees, but also the common asylum policy and mutual trust. the dutch government will now resolve these violations by another unlawful measure by keeping family members separated for several years. if our treatment of refugees measures the shape our rule of law is in, it is in a deplorable state. so governments and commission, stop this race to the bottom. respect the right to asylum. share responsibility. make europe credible again.

rapporteur. – madam president, my thanks to colleagues for the debate and i also want to thank the shadow rapporteurs of the other groups for the intense discussions we had. but in the end, we found each other. we have a broad majority for the call for a clean, competitive and resilient european industry, and that we need to be more strategic. and there are some clear messages in our reports to the commission. there cannot be a green deal without industry policy. if we want to deliver on the growth strategy that the green deal promised, we need industry policy, and the clear message we have for the commission is, come forward with the transition pathways of the industrial ecosystems. give us the pathways that show what is needed in the sectors for the energy transition, for remaining competitive, for what kind of breakthrough technologies are needed, how the regulatory burden on the ecosystems is. and we really call on the commission to come to parliament with these pathways and then have also an annual debate on where we are on these pathways so that we can monitor where we are with the sectors, because industry policy, in the end, is also about adapting measures that seem to work or seem not to work and making sure that we act swiftly, that our european industry will become more resilient, it will become more competitive on the pathway we have. so for us, as parliament, this report is not necessarily an end of the process after reacting to your various industry strategies. we need to stop updating the industry strategy all the time. we need to have an annual debate on the pathways of our ecosystems. for us, this is not the end of the process, it’s the start of cooperation between us and you to make sure that our industry will thrive, that we achieve the goals we set in the green deal but that we do that with a competitive and clean and resilient industry. so thank you very much, i’m looking forward to the cooperation.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection. – madam president, dear colleagues. i would like to comment in particular on procurement, which is within the imco remit. i welcome the provisions where our imco opinion was improved in a practical way. for example, the eu-exclusive procurement, meaning that countries would not be able to negotiate with different manufacturers in parallel. for this issue, a great compromise was found, which is that only after assessment it is decided if it is an eu-exclusive procurement. and this makes sense, since the eu exclusive procurement would be beneficial regarding counter-measures involving plenty of options on the market, for example with tests, but would not be beneficial for other measures where there might be a shortage, for example with vaccines. looking at the final text, although we certainly did not get everything we wanted from the greens efa and imco side, i still welcome this regulation which will surely contribute to strengthening the union's coordinated response to public health emergencies.

executive vice-president of the commission. – honourable members, it is a pleasure to be here to take part and to witness the conclusion of the legislative procedure when it comes to the common charger for portable electronic devices. the common charger will simplify the lives of europeans. no more obsolete chargers piling up in drawers, reducing costs for european citizens; no need to buy a charger for each device. and this will represent savings of at least eur 250 million annually. last but not least, it will contribute to the reduction of more than a thousand tonnes of e-waste every year. please allow me to thank the entire european parliament and in particular, of course, the rapporteur agius saliba of the committee for the support, the tremendous work done which allowed us to finalise the process within just nine months in total. the enlargement of the scope of this initiative, as proposed by the european parliament, i think was well done. it has increased the cover to the broadest possible range of what we call ‘radio equipment’ and even increase the expected benefits for eu citizens and, of course, the environment. and for this reason, i really welcome what was edited: adding e-readers, keyboards, computer mice, portable navigation systems, earbuds, laptops to the scope of devices concerned. and the scope already included quite a range: handheld mobile phones, tablets, digital cameras, headphones, headsets, handheld video game consoles and portable speakers. so it is a lot. but the parliament has also agreed to a transition period of 24 months after the entry into force for all devices and 14 months for laptops. because these transition periods also allows for manufacturers to adjust their supply chains, to adapt to the legislation. and these important texts that have been developed together are not, however, the end of the story. no, we will need to monitor the evolution of charging technologies. we will look at whether or not there is a need to improve consumer convenience, save costs, improve environmental performance. that will allow us to consider extending the scope of devices covered if justified, addressing wireless charging technologies, but also assessing possible further unbundling of charging cables. to conclude, i want to thank the parliament for its commitment to achieve this common charger. i think it is a very good demonstration of the parliament’s attachment to make things simpler. also the relationship between products, consumers and the environment, and that is indeed needed. i hope this is a token of more to come because we are looking forward to future steps and, together with parliament, continuing to improve our everyday life and, of course, our environment, while still allowing new technologies to develop.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, commissioner, alex, colleagues, the main motivation for the adoption of this legislation is to make life easier for millions of european users of electronic devices, not just mobile phones. we've waited long enough for the market to regulate itself. now is the time to act. i'm happy that after the abolition of roaming charges in the european union we will vote on the single charger. with this act, the european parliament has once again demonstrated its ability to respond to citizens' needs and to make their daily lives easier. i thank the rapporteur, mr alex aliva, all the colleagues with whom we have worked on this dossier, and the commission. thank you for the fact that, by working together, we have been able to extend the scope of this act. i'm not going to repeat what my colleagues said about what devices we're talking about. this is a huge achievement for the european union. it creates convenience and saves money for citizens, while benefiting the environment by reducing the generation of e-waste. at the same time, we're not stopping scientific and technological progress, and we're going to be regularly taking stock and adapting the technology, including the one wireless charger that alex mentioned earlier. we will also look at the possibilities of introducing the single charger for many other areas of the home and household, wherever there are no technical restrictions on the use of the single charger. let's also not forget that by this act we are also setting a standard for the world. i'm pretty sure that many other parts of the world will follow our act, so again the european union is leading the way. with this legislation, we give consumers freedom. they will have the opportunity to choose whether to buy a product with or without a charger. finally, and not least, labels on packaging for devices will be simple and clear enough for citizens to be informed of exactly what they are getting. thank you all again. i think it's a very good day for european consumers.
on behalf of the s&d club. honourable chair, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to thank our rapporteur alex, who fought to make this proposal more relevant to citizens than to producers. the fact is that after more than ten years we are introducing a single charger and that is mainly thanks to the meps who pushed this idea in the interest of the citizens even when no one wanted to hear it. my first debate on this topic was in 2013 when croatia just entered the european parliament. according to my survey, as many as 92.9% of croatian citizens support the introduction of a single charger for small devices. it's nice to keep a promise, even if it takes a long time. in the fall of 2024, phones, headphones, keyboards, tablets, navigation systems, mice, digital cameras, speakers, e-readers and portable game consoles will all have the same charger. and then it's going to be laptop and other devices. since each of us has a drawer full of chargers at home, and there are over 30 types of chargers for mobile phones alone on the market, the eu market, this proposal will bring big savings. it's estimated that we spend over two billion euros a year on new chargers. this way we'll have a choice and a chance to save. we need to ensure that both producers and retailers maintain the different options available to consumers. each year, more than 400 million small chargers are sold in the european union, and more than 11 tonnes of electronic waste is thrown away. we can't spend and throw away like we have three planets at our disposal. this proposal is a major contribution to more sustainable consumption and will help the health of our planet. i call on all of you to vote in favor of this bill for the benefit of consumers and the environment.
on behalf of the renew group. – madam president, commissioner, dear colleagues, one could say, ‘one charger? big deal!’, but this one will positively mark our everyday life. because big projects like the common market consist of small bricks, sometimes very important bricks. and this one common charger is one such important element of our common market. as we all know, it was not easy. it took some time. i also campaigned for this common charger for many years. now finally we have it. we achieved a solution which will facilitate consumers’ lives, and it will help the environment. so it is time for thanks. first, commissioner thierry breton, who demonstrated political courage and came up with a legislative proposal after many years of the commission being too patient with it equipment manufacturers. the commission followed calls coming from us, from the european parliament and from the european citizens: thank you. thank you to alex saliba and the other colleagues – other shadows, especially – who cooperated on this big project, which is one of the bricks of the common market. big thanks for working together so well and helping make the proposal of the commission even more ambitious. we managed to expand its scope and cover more almost twice as many product categories as were initially proposed. and now the common charger will really have an impact on the comfort of our lives, and what is probably most important to me and millions of other citizens is that we will decrease the use of raw materials. there will be less e-waste, i think, by thousands, if not millions of tonnes, and we will give our fellow citizens a chance – which you also underlined – to choose whether you want to buy a new device with a charger or without a charger. and this we always enjoy – choice. so enjoy, and thank you!
on behalf of the ecr group. - madam president! - i have to go. ladies and gentlemen! europeans have more and more portable electronic devices in their homes, pockets or backpacks. it's not just smartphones anymore, but it's tablets, e-book readers, and laptops. the standardisation of charging standards will make them easier to use, cheaper and will reduce the generation of e-waste and the use of rare metals. the ecr group has supported this project from the outset and has supported the expansion of the list of devices that will be equipped with a usb-c socket. we also pointed out that consumers need to be properly informed that a common charging port does not mean that the same charger will be perfect for every device. the graphic design system we are proposing, which the commission is to design, will help to purchase the charger that best suits our needs. we also wanted to give customers the option of buying a new device with or without a charger for as long as possible. we also understand that research into more efficient charging technologies, including wireless charging, must continue and we expect the commission to monitor and support innovation in this area. and congressman alex salib deserves a very special thank you.
for the group the left. madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i am very pleased that we now finally have the opportunity to bring the fight for uniform chargers for mobile phones and many other electronic devices to an end. and it certainly wasn't a short fight. for more than a decade, we have been trying to push the commission to take action and come up with a proposal for the introduction of single chargers from the european parliament. eight years ago, in 2014, we adopted the radio equipment directive, in which we called on the commission to introduce single chargers by means of a delegated act, but until recently the commission has been inactive in this regard. but the problem of different chargers for different electronic devices is one we all face. our homes then accumulate chargers, most of which are designed for their specific device. and that's not only opaque, but it's also unnecessarily expensive for consumers and really very unfriendly to the environment. if the commission really cares about the environment, the steps to introduce a single charger should have been taken long ago. why, then, has she only recently come to grips with the problem? was she afraid to stand up to the interests of companies like apple or amazon? was she afraid of facing possible opposition to the government of former president of the united states of america, donald trump? i would like to remind you that the commission is supposed to defend the interests of the union, not the us government or multinational corporations. and i very much hope that from now on the commission will have a backbone and will no longer be afraid to really start defending the interests of the people who live in the union. yes, i think the ambition contained in the draft directive could have been higher. it could have been applied to many more devices. yet the adoption and standardization of the usb-c standard in two years is a really important step in reducing electronic waste and will undoubtedly lead to an easier life for all of us. as a shadow rapporteur, i therefore hope that the directive will be adopted, and i would like to thank the rapporteur in particular for his work.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. a common charging cable for small electrical appliances in the european union is certainly good news. it is good news because the uniformity or standardization of technical standards can concentrate competition on the places where the citizens, the users, benefit the most and where we can simultaneously generate all the positive side effects already described. i am very pleased that our colleague, mr toine manders, who had the idea back in 2009 when mrs weiler was presenting her report, is here today. it is important that we are prepared to enforce technical standards even if they are initially criticised in industry and in business. we did this with the gsm standard in europe 20 years ago, 30 years ago, when we actually organized competition in the telecommunications market through the devices and not through the standards, as was the case in the united states at that time. unfortunately, ladies and gentlemen, the telecommunications market in europe has lost a great deal since then. it is therefore important that we think about how we can get there more competition and also more attractive markets. the report we are debating today is certainly an important element in this. so, thank you very much to all those who support him.
madam chairman! i'm not going to tell you. we've been talking about it for a long time, as we just heard. in fact, almost as long as i've been a member of this house. but now it's finally happening: we're getting this common charger for our electronic devices, and i think we can be proud of that. i think we've all been in that very frustrating situation; the cell phone ran out of power, and the charger jack we borrowed didn't fit. or when we're traveling, and we're struggling to find space for our cell phone chargers, our headphones, our camera, our laptops, our tablets, and so on. an endless jungle of charging plugs and cables. we're gonna put an end to this. lightning or usb b, or whatever the different charging connectors are called, is being replaced by a single charger type in the form of usb c. and consumers will be able to choose for themselves whether they want to buy a new product with or without a charger. so life is getting easier, and it's good for the environment, and it's good for our wallets. a common charger will not only reduce our ecological footprint, it will also save consumers money. more specifically - we heard this earlier today - we will reduce our electronic waste by about 11 000 tonnes a year, while consumers will save in the region of eur 250 million a year. money that could be used to fill other holes in the household budget. so the legislation we have here is a win-win. the only people who are losing out on this are the greedy companies that have been allowed to capitalize on the many different chargers for too long. i would also like to thank our rapporteur, mr alex saliba, for this. it is a fantastic job you have done and i am convinced that the legislation will serve both the environment and the consumers very well.
madam president, we made it after many years of memorandums of understanding, after years of asking the commission to act, we made it. the common charger is an example of the european union making people’s lives easier: no more searching for the right cable, while also protecting the planet from unnecessary electronic waste. consumers will have a choice: the choice to buy a new device with or without a charger. more consumer choice should always be our goal. at the same time, our decision today should not prevent future innovation. usb-c is the most efficient form of charger today, but usb-d may come tomorrow, or a new wireless standard. european legislation needs to dynamically adapt and not stifle innovation, so the commission must ensure that we amend this legislation as soon as technological developments merit it. this is good progress today, but let’s make sure it doesn’t hold us back in the future.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the escalation in the prices of raw materials and energy costs which we have been witnessing for months is having irreversible consequences, the first victims of which are our farmers, once again. in the face of this emergency, the union must urgently put in place an ambitious and concrete response to halt the run on fertiliser prices and ensure adequate availability for our farms. in fact, 50 percent of our food is based on the use of high-quality fertilisers, which over the past few decades have played a key role in the green revolution of our agricultural system, increasing yields and increasing global food security. now, commissioner, we cannot afford to back down. the situation of the fertiliser industry, which is often forced to shut down its production lines because of unsustainable price increases, requires us to take decisions that can no longer be postponed. the commission has already made a number of proposals, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. at the same time, to ensure the competitiveness and production capacity of the union's agri-food sector, we must invest in all available alternatives and eliminate those constraints, such as the 170 kg nitrogen per hectare per year threshold, which still limit the use of organic fertilisers and the development of true circular economy systems based on the exploitation of digestate. in the face of this emergency, which affects european countries, businesses and households in unequal ways, a european response is therefore needed that can support our production system and guide it towards ever greater sustainability and independence of the most critical productions, from gas to ammonia, necessary for the production of fertilisers, to agricultural raw materials that guarantee our food security.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your report. i'm surprised at this debate. for years we have accepted that the fertilisers come from russia, from belarus, for years we have done nothing against the dependence on fossil fuels in agriculture, especially from russia. and now there are people in parliament who are calling for a subsidy for the fertiliser industry. mr. lins, i'll tell you this: yara, the largest nitrogen producer, has jumped from 775 million to 1.5 billion in profits. why should we support this company? kali + salt has made a profit jump. so this is the wrong way to go. we have to get away from these fossil structures and not subsidize them afterwards. the commission itself has put forward a 20% reduction in fertiliser in its 'from the farm to the table' strategy. why would you do that? because in many regions we have a problem of over-fertilization and not of too little fertilizer, too much fertilizer in many waters and too much nitrogen in agriculture. that's why the 20% less fertilizer strategy is so right. we also have to recognize that the nitrogen industry alone contributes to 2.4 percent of global greenhouse gas emissions, and nitrous oxide emissions are rising. so we have to switch to agroecological systems. any farmer can grow legumes, anyone! i do not understand why the commission is suspending the crop rotation, why it is not taking advantage of the possibility that every farmer also grows legumes. we must have sensible crop rotations, but we must also deal better with organic fertilisers. we have a problem in many regions with too much fertilizer. the answer to this question is not to subsidize chemicals more, but to turn to agro-ecological systems. they're future-proof, and they make us independent of both russian gas and big chemical companies.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i agree with the commission that there is an urgent need for a european strategy on fertilisers. it is urgent to help our farmers to obtain them at an affordable price. it is important for them, but also for ensuring our food security and that of third countries. i regret, however, the mistakes made by the commission on this matter. on 10 august, it published guidelines in which it decided to block the export of russian agricultural fertilisers. the embargo was in contradiction with an international agreement signed on 22 july under the auspices of the un. this situation led the un secretary-general to protest to mrs von der leyen. however, it was not until 19 september and pressure from several eu member states that the commission lifted the fertiliser embargo. moral: we have lost five weeks, during which we have made the fertilizer crisis worse instead of trying to solve it. the strategy presented to us today is therefore welcome, but it is a long overdue one. this fact reinforces my conviction that the commission is unable to deal with crises of any kind. in any case, let us move forward: i take note of what the commissioner for agriculture has just said, i share his lucid assessment of the seriousness of the fertiliser crisis, but i do not think that the financial aid to farmers he has presented is sufficient to deal with it. i am waiting for a real strategy, which will make it possible, among other things, to bring home the manufacture of the fertilisers we need.
president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, fertilisers are crucial to our food supply. without nutrients, there is no growth and therefore no harvest. so now that fertilizer prices are escalating, action is needed. not only is the future of many businesses at stake, but global food production is also at stake. however, an important solution is available. i'm talking about recycling nutrients through animal manure and processed manure. renure products based on processed manure meet strict criteria and should therefore be recognised as manure substitutes without delay. the european parliament made a very clear call in its resolution of march. does the commission realise how strategic our food production is, as are the raw materials needed for it? we are now seeing the problems of being dependent on third countries for our energy supply. we must ensure that we do not become dependent on imports from third countries for our fertilisers and food supplies. i therefore urge the commission to approve the replacements for fertilisers mentioned immediately. and that also fits very well with the drive towards a circular economy. in addition, everything must be done to keep fertiliser production in europe affordable.
madam president, colleagues, commissioner, the projections show us that during the coming winter, people in this union will die from hunger and cold, and more and more people will lose their home and fall into extreme poverty. also, homelessness has life—threatening consequences. but, so far in this house, we have seen more support for companies struggling with the rising energy costs than support for citizens. and this abstract number of almost 100 people in the eu at risk of poverty in reality means freezing rooms, empty lunchboxes, and losing your home. that’s why i ask our european leaders, when discussing how to respond to the energy crisis, to look beyond mere numbers and to take action for people. make sure we won’t lose sight of ending homelessness by 2030, and make sure no one will die from the cold this winter. decide on a european moratorium on evictions and a moratorium on being cut off from energy.
mr president, dear commissioner, today we can finally speak freely about the way frontex was covering up pushbacks. despite all attempts to keep it secret, reported violations were disregarded, fundamental rights watchdogs were side-lined, the parliament lied to. the report not only reveals the misbehaviour of a manager, but also the complete failure of the governance of frontex. why did the member states and commission allow this misconduct to go on for years? why didn’t they prevent this deeply rotten culture? this report is not about an incident or a few people, but about the structural human rights crisis at our borders and within frontex, an agency that must guarantee our values and rights. lives, protection, human dignity are at stake, we must see that the lessons learned at all levels. it would be a big mistake to pretend that almost all problems are already solved. no, we must work hard on the structure and culture of the agency on transparency and accountability, and to stop any acceptance of pushbacks.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the eu's energy policy is not undermined by the current crisis. say, almost everything you buy online or offline in the store, it ends up with you via the ocean. and 99 percent of the fuels that power these container ships are fossil fuels, and so bad for climate warming and bad for the air quality in ports. and here it comes! nowhere in the world have we reached an agreement on how to stop this pollution. until now, in the eu. the clean shipping act will ensure that all container ships and all cruise ships sailing in or coming into the eu must be sustainable in the future. with this law, we are making fair agreements on how container ships and cruise ships can emit less and less co2 from 2025 and from 2030 be obliged to use electricity when they are at anchor in the ports, so that there is no more air pollution in seaports such as rotterdam and amsterdam. because every year 400 000 people die prematurely in the eu because of air pollution. what i am particularly proud of is that this law makes the commission's original proposal even more ambitious. that was my mission as a negotiator: an even faster reduction in co2 emissions and a mandatory zero-emission fuel rate, so that fuel producers can start producing these most sustainable fuels now. this ambitious clean shipping act will help us meet climate objectives while giving fuel producers a clear picture of the huge investments needed to make this maritime sector sustainable. and with this law, you won't have to worry about whether your sneakers have made it across the ocean sustainably in the future. all you have to do is think about what color you'd like.
mr president, as i stand before you, a dark scenario threatens millions of households this winter. not only is the energy bill rising, but so are the expenses for daily groceries and transportation. and this energy crisis is widening existing social inequalities. during the covid crisis, a new billionaire was added every 30 hours on average. how would that be? who benefits most from this crisis? the rich get richer and the poor get poorer, but we're moving on to the order of the day. the belief in the free market is so strong that few politicians dare to intervene. but now it is also affecting people who until now have barely managed to keep their heads above water, who are not directly dependent on the government for their survival. and what are we gonna do about this? how do we make sure the energy bill is affordable? the price ceiling in combination with capital gains tax is the key, but it is not the only step. hopefully, the heads of government will reach an agreement next friday to tame the unruly energy market. what are you waiting for? now we are buying together and now we are working on market reform! then we can face the winters ahead with a clearer heart.
